2. Planes Architecture: Data, Control & Governance
This section formalizes the three primary planes of the OS Dashboard AI Assistant—Data Plane, Control Plane, and
Governance & Policy Plane—and defines how they interlock across Local, Enterprise, and Hybrid deployments.
Each plane is a conceptual slice of responsibility, not necessarily a separate deployment tier. The same physical component
may participate in multiple planes, but with different roles and guarantees in each.
All higher-order behavior (workspaces, driver fabric, capsules, Meta-Stack, HyperMesh) is built as compositions over these
planes.
2.1 Data Plane – Definition, Responsibilities & Boundaries
The Data Plane is the system’s memory and record of reality. It contains:
• The facts of what exists (projects, documents, code, configurations, environments, twins, capsules, policies).
• The history of what has happened (ledger events, logs, metrics, traces, policy decisions).
• The interpreted structure of those facts (CIR, indices, graphs, archives).
It does not decide what should happen (that is the Control Plane), nor whether something is allowed or economically
acceptable (Governance & Policy Plane). It simply records, serves, and indexes.
2.1.1 Purpose & Responsibilities
The Data Plane is responsible for:
• Persisting core content and state:
○ Projects, tasks, workspaces, Master Stack entries.
○ CIR Documents & Blocks (normalized representations of documents, code, notes, logs, research artifacts).
○ Capsule manifests, Capsule Packs, Blueprints, My Stack Capsules.
○ Digital Twin and Cognitive Twin definitions and state snapshots.
○ Policies, Policy Packs, Safety Harnesses, Compliance Packs (as data objects).
• Persisting temporal & causal records:
○ Project Ledger events (append-only, tamper-evident).
○ Policy evaluations and governance decisions.
○ AI usage and billing events.
○ Environment changes (package installs/upgrades/removals, blueprint convergence, drift detections).
• Persisting observability & telemetry:
○ Metrics (time-series).
○ Structured logs.
○ Traces (cross-component execution paths).
• Providing query surfaces & indices:
○ Full-text search.
○ Semantic/vector search.
○ Knowledge and dependency graphs.
○ Time-series & analytical queries over metrics and events.
• Supporting time-travel, replay & reconstruction:
○ Reconstruct project state and environment “as of” a point in time.
○ Replay workflows and investigate incidents.
○ Provide inputs for research backtesting and Meta-Stack analysis.
Key invariant: All durable state that matters to OS Dashboard must be representable inside the Data Plane and reachable
via structured queries.
2.1.2 Scope & Boundaries
In scope:
• Core content & configuration
○ CIRDocuments & CIRBlocks (docs, code, configs, logs elevated to CIR, research notes).
○ Project and workspace metadata and settings.
○ Capsule manifests, tests, environment specs, execution traces.
○ Twin definitions, parameters, and state histories.
○ Policy & governance definitions and versions.

2.1.2 Scope & Boundaries
In scope:
• Core content & configuration
○ CIRDocuments & CIRBlocks (docs, code, configs, logs elevated to CIR, research notes).
○ Project and workspace metadata and settings.
○ Capsule manifests, tests, environment specs, execution traces.
○ Twin definitions, parameters, and state histories.
○ Policy & governance definitions and versions.
• Temporal & causal records
○ ProjectEvents in the Ledger.
○ Policy evaluation events.
○ Usage and billing records.
• Indices & telemetry
○ Full-text indices over CIR, manifests, metadata.
○ Vector indices over content, code, logs, twins, capsules.
○ Graph indices linking concepts, experiments, capsules, policies, and twins.
○ Metrics, logs, traces.
Out of scope:
• Purely ephemeral computation state:
○ In-process caches, transient prompts, model internal states, short-lived buffers.
• Live workflow execution state:
○ Owned by the Control Plane and reflected into the Data Plane via events, not stored as hidden in-memory
mutable state.
Change discipline:
• The Data Plane is mutated only via Control Plane actions that have been authorized by Governance.
• From a human/regulator perspective, the Data Plane is effectively read-only; write operations are always mediated by
plans, drivers, capsules, and policy.
2.1.3 Content, CIR, Ledger & Knowledge Stores
The Data Plane is composed of multiple logical stores. At a minimum:
1. CIR & Content Store
○ Stores canonical content in Canonical Internal Representation:
§ CIRDocument with metadata and ordered CIRBlocks.
§ CIRBlock for paragraphs, code cells, equations, tables, headings, diagrams, annotations, etc.
○ Supports:
§ Version history, branching, and merge metadata.
§ Links back to external sources (Git, SaaS docs, DB snapshots) when applicable.
§ Classification tags:
□ Sensitivity (public/internal/confidential/PHI/PCI/etc.).
□ Domain/workspace tags.
□ Compliance tags.
○ Used by:
§ Cognitive Layer (context assembly and reasoning).
§ Workspaces (Dev, Research, Writer, Finance, Cyber, Twins).
§ Evidence generators and auditors.
2. Project & Workspace Store
○ Models:
§ Projects, Tasks, Workspaces, Master Stack node(s).
§ Memberships and role bindings (references to Identity layer).
§ Environment Profiles and Blueprint assignments.
○ Links:
§ From Projects/Tasks to:
□ CIR content,
□ Capsules,
□ Ledger events,
□ Environment specs,
□ Twins and policies.
3. Capsule & Environment Store
○ Stores:
§ Capsule manifests, Capsule Packs, Meta-Capsules.
§ Blueprints and My Stack Capsules.
§ Tests, validation results, quality scores, trust scores.
§ Marketplace metadata (publisher, version, pricing, ratings).

□ Ledger events,
□ Environment specs,
□ Twins and policies.
3. Capsule & Environment Store
○ Stores:
§ Capsule manifests, Capsule Packs, Meta-Capsules.
§ Blueprints and My Stack Capsules.
§ Tests, validation results, quality scores, trust scores.
§ Marketplace metadata (publisher, version, pricing, ratings).
○ Environment artifacts:
§ Environment specs (OS, package lists, tool versions).
§ Snapshots / diffs of actual machine state.
○ Responsibilities:
§ Reproducible Capsule runs.
§ Environment drift detection and remediation planning.
§ Catalog of automation building blocks for Operator Studio and Cross-Capsule Composer.
4. Policy & Governance Store
○ Stores:
§ Individual policies and rulesets.
§ Policy Packs and Compliance Packs.
§ Safety Harness definitions for commands, packages, Capsules, workflows.
○ Provides:
§ Versioned policy snapshots for historical reconstruction.
§ Linkage to policy evaluation events and Evidence Packs.
5. Project Ledger Store
○ Append-only or hash-chained store of ProjectEvents:
§ Human actions (edits, merges, approvals, overrides, policy updates).
§ Agent/Capsule actions (runs, driver invocations, twin manipulations).
§ System events (health changes, drifts, auto-remediations).
§ Policy decisions (allow/deny/require-approval/transform).
○ Supports:
§ Per-project timelines.
§ Cross-project causal graphs.
§ Forensic reconstruction (Record Auditor & Logbook).
§ Backtesting of strategies and policies.
6. Twin & Research Store
○ Digital Twin / Cognitive Twin Store:
§ Structure, parameters, and state snapshots for systems, orgs, teams, and individuals (where allowed).
○ Research Store:
§ Datasets, models, experiments, simulation outputs, fitted parameters.
○ Links:
§ To CIR (papers, notes, reports).
§ To ledger events (runs, experiments, validations).
§ To policies (governance of experiments and twin use).
Interfaces & Contracts
• Exposed as internal services (or library interfaces) to:
○ Application/Orchestrator.
○ Workspaces (Dev, Research, etc.).
○ Cognitive Layer (AIC, personas, daemons).
○ Governance & Billing services (read-only or write via events).
• Limited, read-only subsets exposed via:
○ Regulator Fabric.
○ Federated cross-OS/HyperMesh protocols (later).
Security / Compliance Notes
• All stores carry:
○ Tenant ID, project ID, classification, residency and retention labels.
• Storage layer enforces:
○ Encryption at rest and in transit.
○ Tenant isolation boundaries.
○ Retention and archival rules configured per data class.
2.1.4 Indices (Search, Vector, Graph) & Observability Stores
To make the Data Plane efficient and useful for reasoning, planning, and observability, primary stores are complemented by

• All stores carry:
○ Tenant ID, project ID, classification, residency and retention labels.
• Storage layer enforces:
○ Encryption at rest and in transit.
○ Tenant isolation boundaries.
○ Retention and archival rules configured per data class.
2.1.4 Indices (Search, Vector, Graph) & Observability Stores
To make the Data Plane efficient and useful for reasoning, planning, and observability, primary stores are complemented by
index and telemetry services.
Index Families
1. Search Indices
○ Full-text indices over:
§ CIR content.
§ Project metadata.
§ Capsule manifests and policy docs.
§ Selected ledger event payloads and logbook entries.
○ Provide:
§ Ranked search for user queries.
§ Retrieval for context bundling in cognitive planning.
§ Filters by project, workspace, actor, classification, time, etc.
2. Vector / Semantic Indices
○ Embedding-backed indices over:
§ CIR blocks (text, code).
§ Logs and error messages.
§ Capsule descriptions and twin states.
§ Research artifacts and knowledge snippets.
○ Used by:
§ AIC and personas for retrieval-augmented reasoning.
§ Archivist and Research daemons for cross-project resonance.
§ Meta-Stack engines (Theory Discovery, Canon of Truth).
3. Graph Indices
○ Knowledge and dependency graphs:
§ Nodes: concepts, hypotheses, models, datasets, experiments, Capsules, policies, twins, environments.
§ Edges: supports, refutes, refines, depends_on, implements, runs_on, version_of, derived_from.
○ Capsule & Workflow graph:
§ Capsules, drivers, environments, policies, twins, and their relationships.
○ Used for:
§ Research & Simulation workflows.
§ Impact analysis (e.g., “which Capsules depend on this environment?”).
§ Structural planning by Sora and Meta-Stack.
Observability Stores
1. Metrics Time-Series
○ CPU/memory/I/O/latency/error metrics for:
§ Services, drivers, Capsules, daemons, models.
§ Environment drift counters, health indicators, cost metrics.
○ Time-series DB or equivalent backend.
2. Structured Logs
○ Logs from:
§ Services, drivers, daemons, security components.
§ System Execution Layer commands (stdout/stderr metadata, exit codes).
§ Policy Engine and Governance decisions.
○ Data Plane decides which logs are:
§ Promoted to CIR or ledger as first-class content,
§ Retained purely as observability.
3. Traces
○ Distributed traces across:
§ Intent → reasoning → Capsule workflow → drivers → data stores.
○ Provide:
§ End-to-end timing and dependency analysis.
§ Critical for SRE workflows and HyperDaemon.
Responsibilities
• Provide low-latency reads for:
○ Cognitive planning and context assembly.

3. Traces
○ Distributed traces across:
§ Intent → reasoning → Capsule workflow → drivers → data stores.
○ Provide:
§ End-to-end timing and dependency analysis.
§ Critical for SRE workflows and HyperDaemon.
Responsibilities
• Provide low-latency reads for:
○ Cognitive planning and context assembly.
○ Diagnostics, debugging, and performance tuning.
○ Compliance and forensic investigations.
• Maintain enough historical depth and integrity to support:
○ Evidence Pack generation.
○ Temporal backtesting and replay.
○ Meta-Stack systemic risk and strategy analysis.
2.2 Control Plane – Orchestration, Scheduling & Execution Flows
The Control Plane is the system’s nervous system. It turns:
• Intents → Plans → Driver-level actions,
• Under policy and resource constraints.
It:
• Consumes Data Plane state.
• Consults Governance for permission, risk, and budgets.
• Drives the Driver Fabric, Capsule Runtime, and workspaces.
2.2.1 Purpose & Responsibilities
The Control Plane is responsible for:
• Translating intents into executable plans:
○ From users (GUI, chat, voice, CLI, API).
○ From agents and daemons.
○ From external systems (webhooks, CI/CD, ITSM).
• Managing execution plans:
○ Sequencing steps, satisfying dependencies.
○ Handling retries, timeouts, fallbacks, and compensating actions.
○ Coordinating multi-step, multi-driver, multi-host workflows.
• Scheduling and supervising agents & daemons:
○ Allocating budgets and driver scopes.
○ Enforcing per-tenant/project quotas.
• Ensuring observability & auditability:
○ Emitting ledger events for all material actions.
○ Emitting metrics/logs/traces for health and performance.
• Maintaining workspace semantics:
○ Dev, Research, Writer, Cybersecurity, Finance, Twins, Archive, etc.
2.2.2 Scope & Boundaries
In scope:
• assistant_core Orchestrator:
○ Intent parse, context assembly, plan routing.
• Assistant Sessions:
○ Per-user/agent conversational control loops.
• Workspace Controllers:
○ Domain-specific orchestrators (Dev, Research, etc.).
• Daemon Scheduler & Runtime:
○ Event/time-driven agent flows.
• Workflow & Capsule Engine:
○ Plan graph execution.
• Driver Execution Fabric:
○ Registry, routing, runtime invocation of drivers.
Out of scope:
• Long-term persistence (beyond ephemeral state and job metadata).
• Policy and compliance decision logic (delegated to Governance Plane).
• Identity and tenancy authority (delegated to Security / Identity layer).
The Control Plane always goes through Governance for high-impact actions and goes through Data Plane for durable state.
2.2.3 Dashboard, Assistants, Agents & Daemons

• Driver Execution Fabric:
○ Registry, routing, runtime invocation of drivers.
Out of scope:
• Long-term persistence (beyond ephemeral state and job metadata).
• Policy and compliance decision logic (delegated to Governance Plane).
• Identity and tenancy authority (delegated to Security / Identity layer).
The Control Plane always goes through Governance for high-impact actions and goes through Data Plane for durable state.
2.2.3 Dashboard, Assistants, Agents & Daemons
1. assistant_core Orchestrator
• Central coordinator between:
○ Presentation Layer and Cognitive Layer.
○ Workspaces and Driver Fabric.
○ Governance Plane and Data Plane.
• Responsibilities:
○ Normalize intents into structured internal types.
○ Build Context Bundles from Data Plane (CIR, ledger, indices, env specs, policies).
○ Request driver-aware plans from Cognitive Layer (AIC, personas, TRF).
○ Integrate Governance output (policy decisions, budgets) into the plan.
○ Dispatch approved steps to Workflow Engine and Driver Fabric.
2. Assistant Session Engine
• Maintains per-session state:
○ Active project, workspace, persona, and mode.
○ Local conversation context and view-specific filters.
• Responsibilities:
○ Call the orchestrator for plan proposals and execution.
○ Map user actions (approve/modify/cancel) to updated intents.
○ Ensure the same logical semantics across chat, voice, GUI, CLI.
3. Workspace Engines / Controllers
• One controller per workspace:
○ Dev / Code / CI/CD.
○ Research & Simulation.
○ Writer.
○ Business & Finance.
○ Cybersecurity Guardian.
○ Archive / Continuity / Record Auditor.
○ Operator / SRE / Infra.
○ Digital & Cognitive Twins.
• Responsibilities:
○ Interpret domain signals (commits, test failures, experiments, incidents, transactions).
○ Trigger domain-appropriate Capsules and workflows.
○ Maintain domain-specific invariants (e.g., “no deploy to prod without tests”).
4. Daemon Framework
• Daemons (Echo, Oracle, Critic, Archivist, Billing Optimizer, Security Monitor, Marketplace Curator, HyperDaemon, etc.)
are:
○ Scoped by project/tenant/data/driver sets.
○ Budgeted in tokens, time, and driver calls.
• Scheduling:
○ Event-driven (ledger events, metrics thresholds, security alerts).
○ Time-driven (daily scans, periodic summarization, drift detection).
• Responsibilities:
○ Analyze ledger, metrics, and CIR for patterns.
○ Propose new plans (remediations, optimizations, refactors).
○ Run with ever-present Governance checks and Data Plane logging.
Interfaces
• Orchestrator ↔ Cognitive:
○ (Intent, ContextBundle, GovernanceEnvelope) → (ExecutionPlan(s), Explanations, Alternatives)
• Orchestrator ↔ Driver Fabric:
○ (PlanStep, ExecutionContext) → (Result, Events, Errors)
• Daemons ↔ Data Plane:
○ Read from CIR, Ledger, Metrics.
○ Write tasks, recommendations, Capsule proposals, and events.

Interfaces
• Orchestrator ↔ Cognitive:
○ (Intent, ContextBundle, GovernanceEnvelope) → (ExecutionPlan(s), Explanations, Alternatives)
• Orchestrator ↔ Driver Fabric:
○ (PlanStep, ExecutionContext) → (Result, Events, Errors)
• Daemons ↔ Data Plane:
○ Read from CIR, Ledger, Metrics.
○ Write tasks, recommendations, Capsule proposals, and events.
2.2.4 Workflows, Capsules & Driver Invocations
All concrete work flows through Workflows, Capsules, and drivers.
Plans & Workflows
• Plan:
○ A directed graph or sequence of steps, each step being:
§ A Capsule run,
§ A driver call,
§ A human approval/gate,
§ A decision/branch node.
• Workflow:
○ A named, reusable, versioned Plan template:
§ With parameters, branching logic, retries, compensations.
Capsule Execution
• Capsule is the atomic executable unit:
○ Manifest describes:
§ Inputs/outputs,
§ Required drivers (OS, Unix/System Execution, pkg/env, SaaS, data),
§ Required environment (Blueprints/My Stack),
§ Preconditions/postconditions,
§ Tests.
○ Capsule Execution Manager:
§ Resolves env via Package & Environment Drivers.
§ Resolves driver scopes via Governance.
§ Runs Capsule in sandboxed or host contexts with resource limits.
§ Emits ledger events, logs, metrics, Evidence Packs as configured.
Driver Execution Fabric
• Provides uniform interface for:
○ OS drivers (filesystem, processes, UI automation, network).
○ System Execution Layer:
§ Unix/kernel commands exposed via structured drivers:
□ Generic shell.exec or fine-grained wrappers (e.g., driver.git, driver.ffmpeg, driver.kubectl).
§ Command steps include:
□ Command, args, working dir, environment, time/CPU/RAM limits.
○ Package & Environment drivers:
§ brew/choco/winget/apt/dnf/pacman.
§ pip/conda/poetry/npm/etc.
○ SaaS & Data drivers:
§ Git hosting, MS Graph, Google APIs, DBs, internal APIs.
○ Simulation & Twin drivers:
§ HPC schedulers, lab devices, CAD/CAE tools, twin engines.
Control Plane responsibilities for drivers:
• Schedule:
○ Where and when commands run (local host, bastion, cluster).
• Enforce:
○ Limits (time, resource, concurrency, budgets).
○ Policy decisions from Governance.
• Capture:
○ Outputs, exit codes, runtime stats.
○ Structured events for the Ledger and Observability.
Failure Modes & Recovery (Control Plane view)
• Driver Failure:
○ Mark PlanStep failed with structured error metadata.
○ Attempt retries with backoff where safe.

○ Policy decisions from Governance.
• Capture:
○ Outputs, exit codes, runtime stats.
○ Structured events for the Ledger and Observability.
Failure Modes & Recovery (Control Plane view)
• Driver Failure:
○ Mark PlanStep failed with structured error metadata.
○ Attempt retries with backoff where safe.
○ Consider fallback drivers or degraded workflows.
• Capsule / Workflow Failure:
○ Resume from checkpoints.
○ Run compensating actions (rollbacks).
○ Suggest remediation Capsules.
• Daemon Misbehavior (runaway or over-budget):
○ Enforce budget via Governance.
○ Terminate daemon execution and log event.
○ Optionally disable offending Capsule/driver until reviewed.
Human-in-the-Loop
• For high-risk / regulatory workflows:
○ Insert explicit approval nodes.
○ Provide Plan Previews (drivers, commands, env changes, costs, risks).
○ Record approvals and overrides as ledger events and Evidence Packs.
The Control Plane never directly changes the world; it only does so by driving drivers via Capsules, under Governance
decisions, and with Data Plane recording.
2.3 Governance & Policy Plane – Security, Compliance, Risk & Economic Guardrails
The Governance & Policy Plane is the system’s root of trust and constraint engine. It defines:
• Who/what may do what, where, when, and at what cost.
• Under which legal, regulatory, ethical, and economic constraints.
It mediates all side-effectful actions, ensures they are aligned, compliant, and economically controlled, and generates
auditable decisions.
2.3.1 Purpose & Responsibilities
The Governance Plane:
• Enforces:
○ Identity, authN/authZ (with Identity layer).
○ Data access policies (classification, residency, masking).
○ Driver, Capsule, and workflow usage policies.
○ Cost and budget constraints (AI Billing & Usage Fabric).
• Provides:
○ Explainable policy decisions for each material action.
○ Approvals, overrides, and justifications.
○ Regulator and auditor views.
• Integrates:
○ Security posture and risk scoring.
○ Compliance packs and legal constraints.
○ Economic constraints and trade-offs.
No high-impact operation (e.g., OS commands, package installs, production twin updates, external exports, Meta-Stack
interventions in core OS) may bypass Governance.
2.3.2 Policy Engine, Compliance Packs & Regulator Fabric
Policy Engine
• Central evaluation point for policies governing:
○ Model calls and tool usage.
○ Driver invocations (OS/System Execution, pkg/env, SaaS, data).
○ Capsule deployment and execution.
○ Data access and export, including cross-tenant flows.
○ Meta-Stack operations that influence core OS behavior.
• Policy language (DSL) expresses rules over:
○ Actors:
§ Users, roles, personas, daemons, tenants.
○ Resources:
§ Projects, CIR docs, Capsules, data sources, twins, environments.
○ Actions:
read/write/execute/publish/install/export/simulate/route/bill.

○ Meta-Stack operations that influence core OS behavior.
• Policy language (DSL) expresses rules over:
○ Actors:
§ Users, roles, personas, daemons, tenants.
○ Resources:
§ Projects, CIR docs, Capsules, data sources, twins, environments.
○ Actions:
§ read/write/execute/publish/install/export/simulate/route/bill.
○ Context:
§ Time, region, device class, risk score, incident status, budget state, compliance mode.
• Decisions:
○ ALLOW, DENY, REQUIRE_APPROVAL, WARN, TRANSFORM (e.g., redact PII before LLM call) or LOG_ONLY (for
low-risk auditing).
• All decisions:
○ Persisted as policy evaluation events in Ledger.
○ Traceable to policy versions and rule IDs.
Compliance Packs
• Curated policy sets for:
○ GDPR, HIPAA, SOX, PCI-DSS, ISO 27001, SOC2, CCPA, NIST, EU AI Act, sector-specific laws.
• Define:
○ Data locality and residency requirements.
○ Logging and retention obligations.
○ Restrictions on data categories and automation behaviors.
○ Evidence requirements per action class (e.g., Evidence Packs for production changes).
Regulator Fabric
• Dedicated interfaces and roles for:
○ External regulators, internal auditors, compliance officers.
• Provides:
○ Read-only, policy-filtered access to:
§ Ledger slices,
§ Evidence Packs,
§ Policy definitions and evals,
§ Capsule manifests and runs.
○ Policy simulation (“what-if” under alternative packs).
○ Regulation Capsules that test system behavior against regulation-encoded rules.
2.3.3 Budgeting, Cost Governance & Billing Hooks
The Governance Plane integrates tightly with the AI Billing & Usage Fabric.
Cost Governance & Budgets
• Policies specify:
○ Per-tenant, per-project, per-workspace budgets for:
§ Model tokens and calls.
§ Driver invocations (e.g., DB queries, HPC jobs, long-running simulations).
§ Environment operations (package installs/updates, cluster scale-ups).
§ Storage and telemetry retention.
• Enforce:
○ Hard limits (block further actions).
○ Soft limits (warnings, approvals, or delayed execution).
○ Adaptive routing to:
§ Cheaper models for exploratory tasks.
§ Slower batch pipelines instead of ad-hoc high-cost workflows.
Decision integration
• Before executing high-cost plans:
○ Orchestrator obtains:
§ Cost envelope and budget state from Billing & Governance.
§ Possible alternative (cheaper) plan variants suggested by Billing Optimizer daemons.
○ Governance returns:
§ ALLOW, DENY, REQUIRE_APPROVAL, or DEFER/SCHEDULE.
• Approvals for over-budget actions:
○ Bound to finance or admin roles.
○ Logged as events with justification fields and Evidence Packs.
Data Plane & Reporting

○ Orchestrator obtains:
§ Cost envelope and budget state from Billing & Governance.
§ Possible alternative (cheaper) plan variants suggested by Billing Optimizer daemons.
○ Governance returns:
§ ALLOW, DENY, REQUIRE_APPROVAL, or DEFER/SCHEDULE.
• Approvals for over-budget actions:
○ Bound to finance or admin roles.
○ Logged as events with justification fields and Evidence Packs.
Data Plane & Reporting
• Usage and billing records:
○ Written into the Data Plane as first-class entities.
○ Used for:
§ Dashboards and forecasts.
§ Cognitive Economy Engine & Strategy Garden (higher layers).
§ Per-Capsule and per-driver cost attribution.
Governance thus acts as joint safety, compliance, and economic governor.
2.4 Cross-Plane Flows & Invariants (How Planes Interlock)
Behavior is constrained by cross-plane invariants that connect Data, Control, and Governance.
1. Every side-effectful action is governed and logged.
○ Any operation that:
§ Modifies CIR, code, configs, envs, or twins.
§ Executes commands via System Execution Layer.
§ Calls external services or writes to external systems.
○ Must:
§ Be represented as a Plan/Workflow step in Control Plane.
§ Be evaluated by Policy Engine (or fall under explicit allowlist).
§ Emit Ledger events (and often telemetry) into Data Plane.
2. No direct writes bypass Control Plane.
○ Data Plane writes come only from:
§ Control Plane actions that were governed.
○ Direct DB/file writes by arbitrary components are prohibited (or strictly sandboxed and logged as exceptional).
3. Planning must be Data-aware and Governance-aware.
○ Plan generation (Cognitive Layer + Orchestrator) uses:
§ Data Plane for state and history.
§ Governance for constraints and budget envelopes.
○ Personas/AIC should avoid proposing plans that are impossible or illegal under the current policy/budget
environment.
4. Governance decisions are themselves data.
○ Every allow/deny/approval/transform decision:
§ Becomes a Ledger event.
§ Can be inspected by Record Auditor, Regulator, and Meta-Stack layers.
○ Policies are versioned and stored in Data Plane, enabling:
§ “Why was this allowed then but denied now?” style introspection.
5. Observability spans all three planes.
○ Control Plane:
§ Emits metrics/logs/traces.
○ Governance Plane:
§ Emits metrics/logs/traces for policy evaluations and approvals.
○ Data Plane:
§ Stores observability and ties it to ledger events and entities.
○ Meta-Stack:
§ Uses the combined signal for systemic analysis.
6. Least privilege and explicit scope.
○ Control Plane components operate within:
§ Explicit driver scopes, policy domains, and data sets.
○ Data Plane access is:
§ Scoped and filtered by Governance, even for internal daemons.
2.5 Failure Domains, Isolation Boundaries & Failure Containment Across Planes
Plane separation defines failure domains and containment strategies.
2.5.1 Data Plane Failures
Examples:
• DB outage or corruption.
• Object store unavailability.

○ Data Plane access is:
§ Scoped and filtered by Governance, even for internal daemons.
2.5 Failure Domains, Isolation Boundaries & Failure Containment Across Planes
Plane separation defines failure domains and containment strategies.
2.5.1 Data Plane Failures
Examples:
• DB outage or corruption.
• Object store unavailability.
• Search/vector index lag or failure.
• Metrics/logs backend overload.
Effects:
• Control Plane may:
○ Lose ability to fetch complete context or write events.
• Governance Plane may:
○ Temporarily lose access to recent usage and policy evaluation logs.
Containment & Strategies:
• Enter read-only or degraded mode:
○ Block destructive or high-risk operations if ledger and audit integrity cannot be guaranteed.
○ Allow limited low-risk actions using cached context (configurable).
• Signal:
○ Health issues to operators via metrics and alerts.
• Prevent:
○ Silent data corruption by treating inconsistent storage as a blocking condition for sensitive operations.
2.5.2 Control Plane Failures
Examples:
• Orchestrator crash.
• Workflow engine overload.
• Daemon scheduler failure.
• Driver worker pool exhaustion.
Effects:
• New plans may not be generated.
• In-flight workflows may stall or be slowed.
• Data Plane and Governance remain intact.
Containment & Strategies:
• Use idempotent PlanSteps and ledger-based checkpoints:
○ On restart, reconstruct state from Data Plane and resume where safe.
• Apply backpressure and prioritization:
○ Throttle non-critical workflows.
○ Prioritize incident remediation and critical business flows.
• Governance may:
○ Temporarily restrict initiation of high-risk or expensive plans until Control Plane stabilizes.
2.5.3 Governance & Policy Plane Failures / Misconfiguration
Examples:
• Policy Engine unavailable or degraded.
• Conflicting policies causing deadlocks or global deny.
• Misapplied Compliance Pack.
Effects:
• Control Plane cannot safely determine allow/deny for new actions.
• Risk of under- or over-restricted behavior.
Containment & Strategies:
• Default behavior:
○ Fail-closed for high-risk operations (system execution, package/env changes, data exports, production twin
mutations).
○ Fail-open or cached-policy for low-risk operations (e.g., read-only, purely local, or tenant-configured exceptions),
with explicit flags and warnings.
• Emergency “break-glass” policies:
○ Allow designated roles to:
§ Override or bypass normal policy evaluation for specific actions,
§ With mandatory justification, heavy logging, and automatic Evidence Packs.
• Safe rollout of policy changes:

○ Fail-closed for high-risk operations (system execution, package/env changes, data exports, production twin
mutations).
○ Fail-open or cached-policy for low-risk operations (e.g., read-only, purely local, or tenant-configured exceptions),
with explicit flags and warnings.
• Emergency “break-glass” policies:
○ Allow designated roles to:
§ Override or bypass normal policy evaluation for specific actions,
§ With mandatory justification, heavy logging, and automatic Evidence Packs.
• Safe rollout of policy changes:
○ Policy simulation/dry-run mode before full enforcement.
○ Staged rollout with monitoring for unintended effects.
2.5.4 Tenant & Execution Isolation
Tenant Isolation
• Data Plane:
○ Per-tenant DBs, schemas, or strong row-level isolation.
• Control Plane:
○ Tenant-aware queues, worker pools, and quotas.
• Governance:
○ Per-tenant policy sets and compliance packs.
○ Explicit cross-tenant flows governed and logged.
Execution Isolation
• System Execution and Package & Env drivers:
○ Prefer execution in containers/sandboxes/bastion hosts.
• Capsules:
○ Namespaced per tenant/project.
○ Denied cross-tenant effects unless explicitly federated with strict policies.
• Meta-Stack operations:
○ Scoped explicitly to allowed sets of tenants/projects; cross-tenant operations require explicit Multi-OS/HyperMesh
governance.
2.6 Plane-Specific SLAs, SLOs & Scaling Patterns
Each plane has distinct non-functional requirements.
2.6.1 Data Plane SLAs/SLOs & Scaling
Focus: Durability, integrity, queryability.
• SLOs:
○ Write durability: no acknowledged Ledger/CIR writes lost under defined failure conditions.
○ Read latency targets:
§ CIR & metadata reads.
§ Ledger/event queries.
§ Search and vector queries.
○ Index freshness:
§ Max lag between commit and searchability.
• Scaling:
○ Horizontal scaling of:
§ Search and vector indices.
§ Metrics/logs infrastructure.
○ Sharding/partitioning by:
§ Tenant, project, or time.
○ Tiering:
§ Hot vs cold storage for cost control (especially logs/traces).
○ DR:
§ Multi-region replication, backups, restore procedures.
2.6.2 Control Plane SLAs/SLOs & Scaling
Focus: Responsiveness and throughput.
• SLOs:
○ Plan preview latency:
§ Time from intent to plan outline or proposed actions.
○ Workflow scheduling latency:
§ Time from scheduled trigger to first step execution.
○ Reliability:
§ Percentage of workflows completed without orchestration-related errors.
• Scaling:

• SLOs:
○ Plan preview latency:
§ Time from intent to plan outline or proposed actions.
○ Workflow scheduling latency:
§ Time from scheduled trigger to first step execution.
○ Reliability:
§ Percentage of workflows completed without orchestration-related errors.
• Scaling:
○ Stateless orchestrator and session services:
§ Auto-scaled based on request volume.
○ Worker pools for Capsules & drivers:
§ Separate pools for interactive vs batch vs heavy compute.
§ Priority queues (human-interactive > batch; incident remediation > background).
○ Tenant isolation:
§ Per-tenant quotas and optional dedicated worker pools to avoid noisy neighbors.
2.6.3 Governance & Policy Plane SLAs/SLOs & Scaling
Focus: Correctness, consistency, high availability for decisions.
• SLOs:
○ Policy decision latency:
§ Upper bound for allow/deny/approval decisions for typical actions.
○ Availability:
§ High uptime targets (e.g., ≥ 99.9%) for enterprise/regulatory environments.
○ Evidence Pack SLA:
§ Time to assemble and return Evidence Packs for requested time windows/workflows.
• Scaling:
○ Stateless policy evaluators:
§ Horizontally scaled with cache for low-risk allowlists.
○ Strongly consistent configuration:
§ Policy and Compliance Pack updates propagate atomically and verifiably.
○ Separate capacity for:
§ High-volume everyday policy checks.
§ Burst workloads (e.g., large approval cycles, regulatory audits).
2.7 Open Questions / TODOs (Planes Architecture)
This subsection tracks unresolved design decisions and planned refinements for the plane model.
1. Ledger Architecture & Cryptographic Guarantees
○ Single global multi-tenant ledger vs per-tenant ledgers vs hybrid.
○ What degree of cryptographic tamper-evidence (hash chains, Merkle trees, external notarization) is baseline vs
tiered?
2. Policy Evaluation Locality & Caching
○ In Local Mode:
§ What portions of policy logic can run fully on-device (e.g., cached Safety Harness for commands) vs
requiring remote evaluation?
○ In Enterprise:
§ Central policy service vs regional satellites; how to maintain consistency for critical rules?
○ How to safely cache policy decisions for low-risk actions without violating compliance?
3. Granularity of Policy Checkpoints
○ Per driver call vs per workflow step vs per Capsule type vs per plan.
○ Trade-offs between:
§ Audit detail/precision.
§ Latency and CPU overhead of policy evaluation.
4. Observability as a Fourth Plane
○ Whether to treat Observability as:
§ A formal “Telemetry Plane” with its own SLAs and governance, or
§ A sub-discipline inside the Data Plane with cross-plane consumers.
○ How this impacts Meta-Stack capabilities like HyperDaemon and Global Risk Fabric.
5. Offline & Intermittent Governance (Local & Edge)
○ In offline Local Mode:
§ What categories of operations can proceed with cached policies and deferred logging?
§ What reconciliation mechanisms exist when connectivity returns?
○ How to mark and treat “offline decisions” in audits and Evidence Packs?
6. Command & Environment Policy Granularity

§ A sub-discipline inside the Data Plane with cross-plane consumers.
○ How this impacts Meta-Stack capabilities like HyperDaemon and Global Risk Fabric.
5. Offline & Intermittent Governance (Local & Edge)
○ In offline Local Mode:
§ What categories of operations can proceed with cached policies and deferred logging?
§ What reconciliation mechanisms exist when connectivity returns?
○ How to mark and treat “offline decisions” in audits and Evidence Packs?
6. Command & Environment Policy Granularity
○ Design of practical Safety Harness schemas for:
§ Unix/System Execution commands and arguments.
§ Package operations and environment changes (per command, per package, per Capsule).
○ Balancing:
§ Expressiveness for security/compliance teams.
§ Usability and maintainability of policy definitions.
7. Data Plane Multi-Model Strategy
○ How strongly to separate:
§ Relational, document, vector, graph, and time-series stores.
○ When to consolidate vs specialize, given performance, cost, and complexity.
8. Multi-OS / HyperMesh Governance
○ For workflows spanning multiple OS Dashboard instances:
§ How is governance federated?
§ Who is the root of trust for cross-org plans?
§ What data is shared across instances for audit vs kept strictly local?
9. Backtesting, Replay & Storage Cost
○ How long to retain:
§ Raw logs and traces vs aggregated summaries.
○ What minimal subset of events is required to reconstruct:
§ Plans, env state, Capsule behaviors, and policy paths.
10. Plane-Aware Cost Models
○ How to attribute cost across planes:
§ Data Plane (storage, indexing, telemetry).
§ Control Plane (compute, scheduling, Capsule execution).
§ Governance Plane (policy evaluation, Evidence Pack generation).
○ How this attribution informs:
§ Product pricing,
§ Internal chargeback,
§ Cognitive Economy optimization.
11. Meta-Stack Feedback Boundaries
○ How aggressively may Meta-Stack capabilities (ARC, HyperDaemon, Canon of Truth, Ontological Compiler, Global
Policy Fabric) be allowed to:
§ Propose plane changes vs directly mutate policies, env specs, or plane configuration?
○ What additional Governance constraints apply to Meta-Stack actions vs normal automation?
These questions will be refined and resolved in later sections (Driver Fabric, Capsule System & Ledger, Security &
Governance, Billing & Usage Fabric, Meta-Stack), and the plane model will remain the architectural backbone they must
respect.
3. Core Domain & Knowledge Model
This section defines the canonical data model of the OS Dashboard AI Assistant.
Everything the system does—reasoning, planning, execution, governance, billing, and meta-analysis—is expressed as:
• Reads from these entities,
• Changes to these entities,
• Or evaluations over these entities.
The model is:
• Shared across all planes (Data, Control, Governance),
• Stable across Local and Enterprise deployments,
• Extensible for higher layers (Meta-Stack, HyperMesh, Reality Twin Mesh, Canon of Truth, etc.).
3.1 Users, Tenants, Identity & Roles
3.1.1 Purpose
Provide a unified identity model for:
• Solo users in Local Mode,
• Multi-tenant organizational deployments,
• Fine-grained RBAC/ABAC for policy, driver access, and audit.
3.1.2 Key Entities
User

3.1 Users, Tenants, Identity & Roles
3.1.1 Purpose
Provide a unified identity model for:
• Solo users in Local Mode,
• Multi-tenant organizational deployments,
• Fine-grained RBAC/ABAC for policy, driver access, and audit.
3.1.2 Key Entities
User
• Human actor or service principal.
• Core attributes:
○ user_id
○ display_name
○ primary_email
○ time_zone, locale
○ personas_enabled / persona preferences
○ security_clearance_tags (e.g., PHI_ALLOWED, SECRETS_ALLOWED)
○ billing_profile_id
○ is_service_account (for daemons, CI, external integrations)
Tenant
• Logical organization / realm; root of multi-tenant isolation.
• Attributes:
○ tenant_id
○ name, legal_entity
○ compliance_profile (e.g., GDPR_STRICT, HIPAA)
○ data_residency_region
○ billing_account_id
TenantMembership
• Relationship between User and Tenant.
• Attributes:
○ membership_id
○ user_id, tenant_id
○ roles (see below)
○ status (invited, active, suspended)
○ created_at, updated_at
Role
• Coarse-grained permission bundle.
• Examples (tenant-level):
○ TENANT_ADMIN, SECURITY_OFFICER, FINANCE_ADMIN, AUDITOR, REGULATOR
• Examples (project-level):
○ PROJECT_OWNER, MAINTAINER, EDITOR, REVIEWER, VIEWER
• Attributes:
○ role_id, scope (tenant / project / workspace), permissions (enumerated actions)
3.1.3 Interfaces & Contracts
• Identity exposed as an Actor:
○ Actor = (user_id, tenant_id, roles, attributes)
• Governance Plane consumes Actor attributes for policy evaluation.
• All ledger events include actor_ref so every change is attributable.
3.1.4 Notes
• Local Mode may collapse Tenant and User (single implicit tenant) but keeps the same conceptual model.
• Service accounts and long-running agents are modeled as User with is_service_account = true and restricted roles.
3.2 Identity, Execution Scopes & Driver Scopes
3.2.1 Purpose
Tie who is acting to what they can do, where, and with which drivers, in a way that is:
• Explicit for planning (Cognitive Layer),
• Enforceable for execution (Control Plane + Driver Fabric),
• Auditable and governable (Governance Plane + Ledger).
3.2.2 Core Concepts
ExecutionScope
The runtime context for any action. At minimum:
• tenant_id
• actor_ref (User or service principal, plus roles)

Tie who is acting to what they can do, where, and with which drivers, in a way that is:
• Explicit for planning (Cognitive Layer),
• Enforceable for execution (Control Plane + Driver Fabric),
• Auditable and governable (Governance Plane + Ledger).
3.2.2 Core Concepts
ExecutionScope
The runtime context for any action. At minimum:
• tenant_id
• actor_ref (User or service principal, plus roles)
• project_id?
• workspace_id?
• capsule_id? (if inside a Capsule)
• env_profile_id? / env_spec_id?
• policy_context (resolved policies / packs)
• budget_context (cost constraints, quotas)
Every driver call and Capsule run executes under a specific ExecutionScope.
Driver
• Logical abstraction over a capability surface:
○ OS driver, Unix / System Execution Layer, Package & Environment driver, SaaS connector, DB driver,
Simulation/Twin driver, etc.
• Attributes:
○ driver_id
○ type (OS, UNIX, PKG, SAAS, DB, HPC, TWIN, etc.)
○ risk_tier (READ_ONLY, MUTATING, PRIVILEGED)
○ operations (supported verbs)
DriverScope
• Binding: (actor, context) → allowed driver set + constraints.
• Context dimensions:
○ Tenant defaults,
○ Project / Workspace,
○ Capsule requirements,
○ Persona / daemon.
Attributes:
• allowed_driver_ids
• operation_constraints (e.g., only read operations)
• resource_constraints (hosts, directories, datasets, systems)
• rate_limits / quotas
• risk_envelope (max allowed risk tier)
DriverProfile
• Reusable template for DriverScopes:
○ e.g., “Read-Only Research Agent”, “DevOps SRE Agent”, “Finance Vault Agent”.
• Applied to:
○ Service accounts, Capsules, personas.
CommandPolicy / SafetyHarness
• Fine-grained policy for Unix/System Execution & package actions:
○ Allowed binaries/patterns/arguments,
○ Allowed paths/hosts,
○ Classification: SAFE, WARN, REQUIRE_APPROVAL, BLOCKED.
3.2.3 Behavior & Invariants
• Driver scopes are composable:
○ Tenant defaults ∧ Project overrides ∧ Capsule manifest ∧ Actor roles.
• Cognitive planning always receives explicit driver_scope in the Context Bundle.
• Driver Execution Fabric:
○ Checks DriverScope and CommandPolicy for every invocation.
○ Submits the candidate action to the Policy Engine (Governance Plane).
• Every change in DriverScopes / DriverProfiles / SafetyHarness is:
○ Logged in the Ledger (who changed what, when, for whom).
• Every driver call:
○ Is executed under a resolved ExecutionScope and recorded as a ProjectEvent.
3.3 Projects, Workspaces, Domains & Environment Profiles
3.3.1 Purpose
Model all work as Projects, each with one or more Workspaces and Environment Profiles, so that:

○ Submits the candidate action to the Policy Engine (Governance Plane).
• Every change in DriverScopes / DriverProfiles / SafetyHarness is:
○ Logged in the Ledger (who changed what, when, for whom).
• Every driver call:
○ Is executed under a resolved ExecutionScope and recorded as a ProjectEvent.
3.3 Projects, Workspaces, Domains & Environment Profiles
3.3.1 Purpose
Model all work as Projects, each with one or more Workspaces and Environment Profiles, so that:
• Cognitive agents have a stable substrate for context,
• Control Plane can plan and schedule workflows,
• Governance can apply scoped policies and budgets.
3.3.2 Key Entities
Project
• Attributes:
○ project_id, tenant_id
○ name, description
○ type (e.g., ResearchPaper, Framework, Product, Book, SystemDesign)
○ domain_tags (physics, dev, writing, finance, security, etc.)
○ status (IDEA, ACTIVE, ON_HOLD, ARCHIVED)
○ root_resources (Git repos, dirs, doc IDs, DBs)
• Relationships:
○ Has many: Workspaces, Tasks, CIRDocuments, LedgerEvents, Capsules, EnvironmentProfiles, Twins.
Workspace
• Domain-specific view/engine bundle attached to a Project:
○ Dev, Research & Simulation, Writer, Business/Finance, Cybersecurity, Archive/Record Auditor, Operator/SRE, Twin,
etc.
• Attributes:
○ workspace_id, project_id
○ kind (enum)
○ enabled_modules (Merge Advisor, Research Orchestrator, Cybersecurity Guardian, Finance Console, etc.)
EnvironmentProfile
• High-level descriptor of the runtime environment required by a Project/Workspace.
• Attributes:
○ env_profile_id, project_id
○ name (e.g., dev, staging, prod, lab)
○ description
○ target_tiers (local, cluster/HPC, cloud)
○ References:
§ One or more EnvironmentBlueprint(s) (see 3.9)
○ risk_level and policy tags (e.g., PROD_LOCKDOWN, SANDBOX_FREEDOM)
DomainTag
• Shared vocabulary for domain classification:
○ PHYSICS, MATH, SYSTEM_DESIGN, FINTECH, FICTION_LORE, etc.
• Used to:
○ Route to appropriate daemons, tools, ontologies, and TRF schemas.
3.3.3 Notes
• Projects are typically the unit of ledgering; tenant-wide or infra operations may use special meta-Projects.
• Enabling a Workspace:
○ Registers additional daemons, UI elements, Capsule sets, and driver needs for that Project.
3.4 Task Model (States, Priority, Ownership, Persona/Agent Owners)
3.4.1 Purpose
Represent granular units of work, unify human and agent responsibilities, and feed Master Stack scheduling and automation.
3.4.2 Key Entity: Task
Task
• Attributes:
○ task_id, project_id, workspace_id?
○ title, description, tags (refactor, bug, experiment, finance, security, etc.)
○ state:
§ e.g., BACKLOG, PLANNED, IN_PROGRESS, BLOCKED, IN_REVIEW, DONE, CANCELLED
○ priority: P0–P4 or numeric
○ owner_actor_id (human, persona, or daemon)

Task
• Attributes:
○ task_id, project_id, workspace_id?
○ title, description, tags (refactor, bug, experiment, finance, security, etc.)
○ state:
§ e.g., BACKLOG, PLANNED, IN_PROGRESS, BLOCKED, IN_REVIEW, DONE, CANCELLED
○ priority: P0–P4 or numeric
○ owner_actor_id (human, persona, or daemon)
○ origin:
§ manual, Commit→Task, Merge Advisor, Research Orchestrator, Cybersecurity Guardian, HyperDaemon, etc.
○ due_date, created_at, updated_at
○ linked_entities:
§ CIR blocks/docs, Ledger events, Capsules, env specs, Twin states
• Relationships:
○ May spawn or be spawned by Capsule runs and workflows.
○ Feeds Master Stack scheduling and Project Intelligence.
TaskBinding
• Optional mapping from Task to automation primitives:
○ Capsule(s),
○ Workflow,
○ Unix command graph,
○ Sandbox/Testbed spec.
3.4.3 Invariants
• Each Task belongs to exactly one Project.
• Auto-generated Tasks always record their origin (event IDs, commits, incidents).
• High-impact Tasks (e.g., env changes, security remediations) must be linked to Evidence Packs and policy decisions.
3.5 Master Stack & Project Intelligence / Scheduling Model
3.5.1 Purpose
Provide a global, cross-project prioritized view of work (Master Stack) and a per-project intelligence layer that drives that
stack.
3.5.2 Key Entities
MasterStackEntry
• Derived entity combining Task, Project, and dynamic scores.
• Attributes:
○ entry_id
○ task_id / project_id / incident_id?
○ computed_priority
○ Score vector: value, risk, urgency, env-health impact, learning impact
○ time_window_hint, effort_estimate
○ Dependency links (prerequisites, blocks)
ProjectIntelligenceSnapshot
• Per-project summary state:
○ Health: failing tests, env drift, open incidents
○ Momentum: recent commits, ledger activity, Capsule runs
○ Financial: cost burn, ROI hints (via Finance console)
○ Knowledge: unresolved contradictions, key frameworks (via Archive & TRF)
3.5.3 Behavior & Invariants
• Sora + Project Intelligence daemons:
○ Continuously re-score entries using ledger, metrics, CIR, env health, budgets.
• Master Stack is materialized as views, not a standalone mutable store.
• Re-prioritization and scheduling decisions are:
○ Logged as ledger events (STACK_ENTRY_SCORED, STACK_REORDERED, etc.).
• Master Stack feeds:
○ Daily focus suggestions,
○ Auto-assignment to personas/agents,
○ Strategic planning and runway analysis.
3.6 CIR (Canonical Internal Representation) – Documents & Blocks
3.6.1 Purpose
Provide a format-agnostic, model-friendly representation of all substantive content:
• Documents, specs, books, notes, code, configs, experimental logs, research outputs, lore, etc.
CIR is the substrate for:
• Retrieval-augmented reasoning,

○ Auto-assignment to personas/agents,
○ Strategic planning and runway analysis.
3.6 CIR (Canonical Internal Representation) – Documents & Blocks
3.6.1 Purpose
Provide a format-agnostic, model-friendly representation of all substantive content:
• Documents, specs, books, notes, code, configs, experimental logs, research outputs, lore, etc.
CIR is the substrate for:
• Retrieval-augmented reasoning,
• Merge/diff, review and commentary,
• Canon of Truth and Knowledge Graph building,
• Evidence Packs and regulatory explanations.
3.6.2 Key Entities
CIRDocument
• Attributes:
○ cir_doc_id, project_id, tenant_id
○ source_system (Git, Google Docs, Word, Notion, Email, DB, etc.)
○ external_ref (URI/ID in source system)
○ title, doc_type (spec, note, paper, script, config, log, email, lore, etc.)
○ Version chain pointers (internal + external)
○ Classification tags (confidentiality, domain, workspace)
• Contains:
○ Ordered list of CIRBlock identifiers.
CIRBlock
• Smallest semantically meaningful unit.
• Attributes:
○ block_id, cir_doc_id, position
○ block_type (paragraph, heading, code, table, list, equation, diagram placeholder, comment, etc.)
○ structure and text / structured content
○ annotations (review comments, semantic tags, ontology refs)
○ Links:
§ To Ledger events that created/modified it,
§ To Capsules (producer/consumer),
§ To policies (e.g., redaction, compliance tags)
CIRView
• Materialized projection of CIR for a persona/workspace (dev view, writer view, regulator view).
3.6.3 Invariants
• Connectors normalize external content into CIR; publishing/syncing back is derived from CIR.
• Cognitive agents reason primarily over CIRBlocks, not raw external formats.
• CIR is fully addressable from Tasks, Ledger, Capsules, Policies, and Evidence Packs.
3.7 Project Ledger, Events, Timelines & Causal Graph
3.7.1 Purpose
Provide a tamper-evident, time-ordered record of everything that happens in a project/tenant:
• Human actions,
• Agent and Capsule actions,
• Driver invocations (including Unix & package),
• Policy decisions,
• Environment changes,
• Twin updates and experiments.
3.7.2 Key Entities
LedgerEvent / ProjectEvent
• Attributes:
○ event_id, project_id, tenant_id
○ timestamp
○ actor_ref
○ event_type
○ payload (typed, structured)
○ prev_event_hash, event_hash (for hash chain)
○ trace_id / span_id (for linking to traces)
• Representative event types:
○ Task lifecycle events.
○ CIR edits and publications.

○ actor_ref
○ event_type
○ payload (typed, structured)
○ prev_event_hash, event_hash (for hash chain)
○ trace_id / span_id (for linking to traces)
• Representative event types:
○ Task lifecycle events.
○ CIR edits and publications.
○ Capsule run start/end.
○ Driver invocations (commands, package actions, SaaS/API calls).
○ Env drift and change applications.
○ Policy evaluations and approvals/denials.
○ Security alerts and incident lifecycle events.
○ Evidence Pack generation.
CausalEdge
• Directed relation between events:
○ caused_by_event_id, depends_on_event_id, reverts_event_id, etc.
• Supports per-project and cross-project causal graphs.
EvidencePack
• Structured bundle:
○ Selected Ledger events,
○ CIR snapshots,
○ Blueprint/MachineProfile snapshots,
○ Policy decisions and explanations,
○ Derived summaries.
3.7.3 Invariants
• Every side-effectful action must yield at least one LedgerEvent.
• High-value flows (releases, incidents, regulatory processes) must be reconstructible from Ledger + snapshots alone.
• Evidence Packs reference LedgerEvent IDs, not ad-hoc logs.
3.8 Knowledge Capsules, Capsule Packs & Capsule Graph
3.8.1 Purpose
Capture executable, reusable knowledge as Capsules:
• Methods, workflows, playbooks, simulations, pipelines, security remediations, finance routines, etc.
Enable composition, governance, marketplace distribution, and self-evolution via Capsule Packs and Capsule Graph.
3.8.2 Key Entities
Capsule
• Attributes:
○ capsule_id, tenant_id, project_id?
○ kind:
§ e.g., TEMPLATE, WORKFLOW, ANALYTICAL, SIMULATION, INFRA, SECURITY, FINANCE, TWIN, TEACHING,
META
○ manifest:
§ Inputs/outputs schema,
§ Required drivers & driver scopes,
§ Required env specs / Blueprints,
§ Preconditions/postconditions,
§ Safety level / risk class, required approvals,
§ Tests and validation checks.
○ version, status (draft, published, deprecated)
○ linked_docs (CIR), evidence_links, policy_tags
CapsuleVersion
• Immutable body of a Capsule at a given version.
• Execution always binds to a specific CapsuleVersion.
CapsulePack
• Bundle of Capsules, Blueprints, and possibly Policies:
○ e.g., “Media Pack”, “Quant Pack”, “Sysadmin & SRE Pack”, “Writer & Publishing Pack”.
• Marketplace metadata:
○ pack_id, domain, dependencies, pricing, ratings, trust level.
CapsuleGraph
• Directed graph of Capsules.
• Nodes: Capsules.
•

CapsulePack
• Bundle of Capsules, Blueprints, and possibly Policies:
○ e.g., “Media Pack”, “Quant Pack”, “Sysadmin & SRE Pack”, “Writer & Publishing Pack”.
• Marketplace metadata:
○ pack_id, domain, dependencies, pricing, ratings, trust level.
CapsuleGraph
• Directed graph of Capsules.
• Nodes: Capsules.
• Edges:
○ calls, depends_on, composes, alternative_for, evolves_from.
3.8.3 Behavior & Invariants
• Capsule versions are immutable; changes require new versions.
• Workflow→App Synthesizer:
○ Converts recorded workflows (driver sequences) into Capsules with manifests/tests.
• Cross-Capsule Composer & Operator Studio:
○ Build higher-order meta-Capsules over the CapsuleGraph.
• Self-evolving Capsule ecosystem:
○ Uses ledger + metrics to propose new CapsuleVersions and graph refactors.
3.9 Environment Blueprints, EnvironmentSpecs & Machine Profiles
3.9.1 Purpose
Model environment state and intent as domain objects, enabling:
• Reproducible environments,
• Drift detection & auto-repair,
• Sandbox/Testbed spawning,
• “Run This Project Here” wizard.
3.9.2 Key Entities
EnvironmentSpec
• Canonical spec of environment requirements for a Project or Capsule.
• Attributes:
○ env_spec_id, project_id?, capsule_id?
○ version
○ Required:
§ CLIs, libraries, runtimes, toolchains (with version pins/constraints),
§ OS constraints,
§ Mappings to package manager names (Homebrew, Chocolatey/Winget, apt/dnf/pacman, pip/conda/npm,
etc.)
○ policy_tags (e.g., ALLOW_INSTALL, REVIEW_REQUIRED, NO_PROD)
EnvironmentBlueprint
• Higher-level environment profile, often role- or domain-oriented.
• Attributes:
○ blueprint_id, tenant_id
○ name, description
○ Domain/role tags (e.g., Backend_Dev, Quant_Research_Dev, Creator_Workstation)
○ base_env_spec_ids (one or more EnvironmentSpecs)
○ Target OS/platforms
○ Optional default Capsule sets and Workspaces
○ Policy classification (who may apply it, where)
MachineProfile
• Snapshot of a real machine/host/cluster node.
• Attributes:
○ machine_id (host fingerprint)
○ owner_user_id?, tenant_id
○ OS and hardware info
○ installed_packages (normalized across managers)
○ runtime_versions
○ configs (select key files/checksums)
○ snapshot_time
○ Blueprint alignment status by Blueprint (IN_SYNC, DRIFTING, UNMANAGED)
EnvDriftRecord
• Captures divergence between desired state (Blueprint/EnvSpec) and observed MachineProfile.
• Attributes:
○ drift_id, entity_ref (Blueprint/MachineProfile),

○ runtime_versions
○ configs (select key files/checksums)
○ snapshot_time
○ Blueprint alignment status by Blueprint (IN_SYNC, DRIFTING, UNMANAGED)
EnvDriftRecord
• Captures divergence between desired state (Blueprint/EnvSpec) and observed MachineProfile.
• Attributes:
○ drift_id, entity_ref (Blueprint/MachineProfile),
○ timestamp, drift_type, severity,
○ proposed_remediation_capsule_id.
3.9.3 Behavior
• Health & Drift Monitors:
○ Periodically compare MachineProfiles to Blueprints/EnvSpecs.
○ Emit EnvDriftRecords, Tasks, and potential Capsule runs for remediation.
• Sandbox/Testbed Spawner:
○ Uses Blueprints to create ephemeral, policy-constrained environments.
• Env changes via Package & Env drivers:
○ Emit ENV_CHANGE_APPLIED LedgerEvents and update MachineProfiles.
3.10 My Stack Capsules & Portable Skill / Setup Sharing
3.10.1 Purpose
Allow users/teams to package their personal or role-specific working environment and practices into a portable artifact.
3.10.2 Key Entity: MyStackCapsule
MyStackCapsule
• Specialized Capsule type.
• Attributes:
○ owner (User or Team)
○ References:
§ One or more EnvironmentBlueprints,
§ Frequently used Capsules and CapsulePacks,
§ Preferred Workspaces and views,
§ Editor/IDE configurations, CLI tools, AI settings.
○ Role tags (optional):
§ PHYSICS_STACK, QUANT_STACK, WRITER_STACK, SRE_STACK, etc.
○ Sharing mode:
§ Private, tenant-internal, or Marketplace.
3.10.3 Behavior
• Applying a MyStackCapsule:
○ Computes environment delta from local MachineProfile.
○ Proposes/install/removes packages and Capsules under policy constraints.
○ Configures Workspaces and UI.
• Used for:
○ Onboarding,
○ Cross-machine portability,
○ “Learn from this expert’s stack” experiences.
3.11 Policies, Policy Packs & Governance Artifacts
3.11.1 Purpose
Express governance logic as data, so Policy Engine and Law-of-the-OS can:
• Enforce rules consistently,
• Provide explanations,
• Generate regulator-grade evidence.
3.11.2 Key Entities
Policy
• Attributes:
○ policy_id, tenant_id
○ scope (tenant / project / workspace / Capsule / driver / data source)
○ rule_dsl (policy language expression)
○ effect (ALLOW, DENY, REQUIRE_APPROVAL, TRANSFORM, LOG_ONLY)
○ priority, enabled, version
• Evaluated over:
○ Actor attributes,
○ Resource attributes,

○ policy_id, tenant_id
○ scope (tenant / project / workspace / Capsule / driver / data source)
○ rule_dsl (policy language expression)
○ effect (ALLOW, DENY, REQUIRE_APPROVAL, TRANSFORM, LOG_ONLY)
○ priority, enabled, version
• Evaluated over:
○ Actor attributes,
○ Resource attributes,
○ Action attributes,
○ Context (time, region, incident state, risk score, budget state).
PolicyPack
• Group of Policies implementing a standard or baseline:
○ GDPR, HIPAA, SOX, PCI-DSS, ISO27001, SOC2, CCPA, NIST, EU AI Act, internal baselines.
• Attributes:
○ pack_id, name, standard, jurisdictions, default scopes.
SafetyHarness
• Specialized policy artifact for commands, packages, and Capsule types.
• May be stored as a dedicated entity or as a Policy subtype.
RegulationCapsule
• Executable policy artifact:
○ Capsules that inspect Ledger, CIR, Blueprints, MachineProfiles, and usage to test compliance.
PolicyDecisionRecord
• Output of policy evaluation:
○ Principal, action, context, decision, policy trace, reason codes.
○ Logged as LedgerEvent and often included in Evidence Packs.
3.12 Usage Records, Cost Entities & Billing Surfaces
3.12.1 Purpose
Model usage and cost as first-class entities for:
• AI Billing & Usage Fabric,
• Governance cost guardrails,
• Optimization (Billing Optimizer, Cognitive Economy Engine),
• Marketplace revenue share.
3.12.2 Key Entities
UsageRecord
• Attributes:
○ usage_id, tenant_id, project_id?, capsule_id?, workspace_id?
○ actor_ref
○ resource_type:
§ MODEL_CALL, DRIVER_INVOKE, COMMAND_EXEC, PACKAGE_ACTION, CAPSULE_RUN, SANDBOX_RUN,
HPC_JOB, STORAGE, etc.
○ Quantities:
§ tokens, seconds, CPU/GPU-minutes, data volume, calls.
○ provider (LLM provider, cloud, cluster, SaaS)
○ unit_cost_estimate, currency
○ timestamp
CostCenter
• Tenant-defined allocation bucket:
○ Teams, products, departments, portfolios.
CostEntity / BillingSurface
• Aggregated view of UsageRecords for a given dimension:
○ Per tenant, per project, per Capsule, per Pack, per Workspace, per CostCenter.
• Used to:
○ Drive dashboards and budgets,
○ Enforce Governance policies (daily/weekly/monthly caps).
RevenueShareRecord
• For Marketplace:
○ Maps usage and revenue to Capsule authors, driver vendors, etc.
3.12.3 Invariants
• All billable actions generate UsageRecords tied back to LedgerEvents.
• Governance decisions about cost (‘deny’, ‘require approval’, ‘downgrade model’) reference Usage and Budget states.

○ Enforce Governance policies (daily/weekly/monthly caps).
RevenueShareRecord
• For Marketplace:
○ Maps usage and revenue to Capsule authors, driver vendors, etc.

3.13 Digital Twins, Cognitive Twins & Other Meta-Entities
3.13.1 Purpose
Provide domain-level representations for:
• Digital twins of systems,
• Enterprise/organizational twins,
• Cognitive twins of humans/roles,
• Nodes in Reality Twin Mesh.
These underpin advanced reasoning, simulation, and meta-stack operations.
3.13.2 Key Entities
DigitalTwin
• Model of a physical or logical system:
○ Device, plant, network, product, process, market.
• Attributes:
○ twin_id, tenant_id, twin_type
○ Structural model (components, states, parameters)
○ linked_projects, linked_capsules, data_sources
○ simulation_models (Capsules or external tools)
EnterpriseTwin
• Special DigitalTwin representing an organization:
○ Process graph, org chart, Capsule network, KPIs, risk surfaces.
• Attributes:
○ enterprise_twin_id, tenant_id
○ capability_graph_ref, kpi_definitions, strategy_capsule_refs
CognitiveTwin
• Behavioral twin of a person, team, or role:
○ Preferences, skills, historical patterns, trust metrics (subject to strict privacy).
• Attributes:
○ cognitive_twin_id
○ user_id? or role_ref
○ learned_patterns (abstracted, not raw logs)
○ delegation_policy (what can be auto-delegated to this twin)
RealityTwinMeshNode
• Node in a larger Reality Twin Mesh:
○ Connects multiple Digital/Enterprise Twins to model ecosystems.
TwinStateSnapshot
• Captured state of a Twin at a point in time.
• Used by:
○ Temporal Backtesting, Multi-Reality Storyboard, HyperDaemon, HyperRegent.
3.13.3 Invariants
• Twin state changes are represented as LedgerEvents and versioned twin states.
• Twins are composite views over CIR, Capsules, EnvSpecs, Policies, and telemetry; they are not isolated data islands.
3.14 Temporal Versioning, Drift Tracking & Reconstruction
3.14.1 Purpose
Enable time-aware reasoning and reconstruction across:
• Projects, knowledge, environments, policies, Capsules, and twins.
3.14.2 Core Concepts
VersionChain
• Applies to:
○ CIRDocuments, Capsules, EnvSpecs, Blueprints, Policies, Twins, MyStackCapsules.
• Events:
○ VERSION_CREATED, VERSION_PROMOTED, VERSION_DEPRECATED logged in Ledger.
DriftRecord
• Generalized drift structure:
○ Env drift (Blueprint vs MachineProfile),
○ Policy drift (declared vs enforced),

• Applies to:
○ CIRDocuments, Capsules, EnvSpecs, Blueprints, Policies, Twins, MyStackCapsules.
• Events:
○ VERSION_CREATED, VERSION_PROMOTED, VERSION_DEPRECATED logged in Ledger.
DriftRecord
• Generalized drift structure:
○ Env drift (Blueprint vs MachineProfile),
○ Policy drift (declared vs enforced),
○ Knowledge drift (canon vs observed practice),
○ Twin drift (expected vs observed behavior/state).
TemporalSnapshot
• Logical snapshot of a Project or Twin at a point in time:
○ Determined by:
§ Ledger prefix up to time T,
§ VersionChains as of T,
§ Optional materialized snapshot optimization.
3.14.3 Behavior & Use Cases
• Reconstruction of:
○ Historical environment via EnvSpecs + package operations.
○ Historical knowledge state via CIR diffs and Capsule versions.
○ Historical governance posture via archived Policies and DecisionRecords.
• Supports:
○ Archive & Continuity workspace,
○ Temporal Backtesting Engine,
○ Canon of Truth (“what did we believe and why at time T?”),
○ Compliance investigations.
